# Interpretation decisions

Every place where the handbook is ambiguous and a human chose a reading. Newest first.
Claude Code appends here after the DGS answers a question; the next DGS reads this before
changing anything.

Decisions marked **DGS** were answered directly by the DGS. Decisions marked **default
(DGS-delegated)** are readings the DGS delegated on 2026-08-31 ("all other questions follow
your default decision") — any of them can be revised by editing this table and asking Claude
Code to update the engine (each has a matching test scenario).

| date | question | decision | decided by | handbook § |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-11 | (Item 13's check against yesterday's parser: on a scanner's own text layer (the OCR bench's L7), item 14's digit-letter-digit code shape for McMaster now reads five real courses whose codes the layer garbled with a digit look-alike — "PHYS 1O1", "MAT 2Z2", "CS 5O00" — rows the parser used to drop. The bench counts each as an extra row.) | **Kept: a real course is shown, flagged, rather than silently dropped.** Every row of a scanner's layer is flagged for the student to check, and the garbled code and title are there to correct; nothing reaches the record unconfirmed. Reading such a code corrected — a look-alike letter between digits read as the digit, with the raw reading beside it, as Batch C (4) does for credits and grades — would extend that answer to course codes, so it is put to the DGS. | default (DGS-delegated) | — |
| 2026-10-11 | (Item 13's full-ladder check: item 14's rule for UCLA's one-digit course numbers alone in their cell read three junk lines of scanned grading keys as rows — Duke's "LP 2.5" read "LP 2S", Tokyo's legend lines "RE 1" and "HPAL 6" — at L3 and L4, levels item 14's L2 / L5 check did not cover.) | **On a scan, a one-digit code needs its credits and a grade.** Each junk row lacks one or the other, while every real UCLA row has both; an in-progress term stands in for the grade. A text layer reads as item 14 left it. Reading the rule on text layers only was the other choice and removes the same three rows, but a scanned UCLA transcript would then lose its one-digit courses. Measured on the saved OCR lines of the whole ladder, L7, the medium set and the pinned pages: three false rows fewer, no regression; the text-path replay unchanged. | default (DGS-delegated) | — |
| 2026-10-11 | (DGS: “Handle … 13” — the OCR flag figures fell at step 2.5 and nobody had checked why; and a row the engine read with confidence was never flagged however wrong it was.) | **Why they fell, then flags on the document's own evidence.** On the same saved OCR lines both figures fell at step 2.5 and nowhere else: its rules made 15 wrong rows right at L2 and 19 at L5, 11 and 18 of them rows the flag had rightly marked, which mostly stay flagged now that they read right. The parser got better; the flag did not get worse. The wrong rows left are mostly confident misreads, so three facts of the document now flag a row on a scan (`evidence`; the ⚠'s hover text says which): a credit value no other row has (four rows or more), a grade cell holding a number or a stray sign, a term whose rows do not add up to its totals line (item 11). A mark of letters alone — McGill's K, NR, WL, Banner's NG — is a mark a school prints, not evidence: flagging it cost 9–11 right rows for 1–2 wrong ones. An empty grade was tried and caught nothing. On the OCR bench (re-parse of the same lines, all documents) flag recall rose 60.9 → 78.3 % at L2 and 59.8 → 80.5 % at L5, and by 18–25 points at every level L0–L6, with precision within 2 points; rows, false rows and field accuracy are unchanged. A text layer is never touched. | default (DGS-delegated) | — |
| 2026-10-11 | (DGS: “Handle … 14” — fix reading errors in the order of their effect on the degree check: fourteen public documents were on the known-failing list, ten needing no DGS decision.) | **Measured first, then fixed in that order.** `npm run audit-impact` (scripts/dev/audit-impact.mts) checks two Ph.D. records per document — the true rows and the rows read — with the saved rules plus an ExternalCourses row ruling every true course transferable, on the same date, and lists every requirement row whose verdict differs and the transfer credit each counts. On the text path three documents changed a verdict — Seoul National University (6 rows, 33 → 9 transfer credits), McMaster (3 rows) and the Ukrainian supplement (3 rows) — and UCLA's credits moved without a verdict; on saved OCR reads 10 of 20 documents at L2 and 8 at L5 (scattered misreads). Fixed, no corpus regression (public exact 154 → 162 of 168): SNU's “4190.669” / “M1522.001000” codes and McMaster's digit-letter-digit numbers (“COMPSCI 1MD3”, “SFWRENG 4G06A/B”); UCLA's one-digit numbers alone in their cell (“PHYSICS 1A”, “ENGCOMP 3”) and a term header ending “IN PROGRESS”; then the families that change no verdict but show the student a wrong term or an extra row — Workday's dated “Winter Term 1” (a winter term starting August–November is that fall, in December the next spring), Colleague's TR rows (transfer credit, left out with the transfer note) and its ungraded current row, Western's second-term suffixes (B, G, S, T) inside a FALL/WINTER session, Tribhuvan's continuously numbered semesters (odd the fall, even the spring), Ladok's four-digit module lines and its issued degree, and Oregon's glued grade symbols (“B>1”, “P*”), its “N No Pass” and its key's “credits are recorded in quarter hours”. BU's pinned expectation for “P*” moves from the raw mark to S — its key defines P* as “Pass, P/F course”. The text path now changes a verdict on one document, the Ukrainian supplement, whose course codes are Cyrillic: reading non-Latin codes is a scope question for the DGS (the app promises English-language transcripts). Still open on the list: McMaster's MOSAIC term column (two rows), and the five needing a DGS decision or none possible (SJTU's country calendar, Sabancı's P, Algeria's code-less rows, Cairo's distant conferral date). | Claude (DGS item 14); the Cyrillic question is the DGS's | CSE §5.2 |
| 2026-10-11 | (DGS: “Handle … 11” — make each import check itself: the readers read a transcript's totals only for its GPA, so a row missed or misread went unnoticed until the student compared the PDF by eye.) | **The rows read are checked against the transcript's own totals, and the preview says where they do not add up.** Recorded only where the figures are unambiguous: Notre Dame's six-figure “Current Term”, “Cumulative” and “Overall” lines (Attempt, Passed, Earned, GPA Hours, Quality Points, GPA) and, from other universities, Banner's labelled term line (“Ehrs … GPA-Hrs … QPts … GPA …”, under the term it closes, scoped to the rows read since that term's header so a combined transcript's two records are kept apart) and its TOTAL INSTITUTION line. Earned hours: a passing grade (A–D±, S, P, CR) earns, F/U/W/I/IP/NR/V do not, a transfer-block row is earned credit for an Overall line. GPA: the letter-graded rows on either common scale (A− 3.667 or 3.7), within 0.01. Not judged: a term with a course in progress (an overall line leaves such rows out), a scope holding a row whose grade is unread. A line is matched against its rows of its level, of its term's level, or all of them — a mismatch only when none adds up. The preview lists each mismatch with both figures and marks the rows of that term (W-CL447–W-CL449); nothing is blocked. Guarded by the corpora: no alarm on any of the 19 public/master's documents that print totals and read exactly, nor on the Notre Dame corpus (tests/totals-check.test.ts, tests/nd-corpus.test.ts); simulated, a missed row is caught on 107 of 118 terms and a misread grade on 107 of 117. OCR imports go through the same reader and so the same check. | Claude (DGS item 11) | — |
| 2026-10-11 | (DGS: “Handle … 12” — measure the Notre Dame import: `npm run replay` scored only other universities' transcripts; and the sanitizer randomized every total, so a sanitized copy could not test a totals check — while it kept each course row's quality points, which gave the original grade away.) | **A Notre Dame corpus and replay lane, fixtures that add up, and a sanitizer that keeps the arithmetic.** `tests/fixtures/nd-transcripts/` (seven documents: the five generator PDFs read through the layout stage, the invented insideND and Banner lists) is pinned by tests/nd-corpus.test.ts and scored by `npm run replay -- --corpus nd` (scripts/dev/score-nd.mts: rows and cells as the other corpora, plus isNotreDame, the cumulative GPA and each level's, the entry term, the degrees); an outside PDF whose expectation says `isNotreDame: true` is read by the Notre Dame parser. The generator's printed totals were wrong and now add up to their rows — the Notre Dame graduate GPA hours were 10 for 9 graded credits (3.300 → 3.667), the undergraduate points 60 for 63.001 (3.750 → 3.938), every insideND term printed one constant line, and the Banner fixture's TOTAL INSTITUTION 19/15/57.99/3.87 for terms adding to 25/21/81.99/3.90. The sanitizer (`scripts/sanitize/rules.mjs` `finish()`) now gives each course row the quality points of its NEW grade, keeps a totals line's hours and recomputes its points and GPA from the rows of its scope (term, cumulative, overall, by level), and leaves a line random when its rows do not add up to its printed GPA hours; replacement grades stay on a common scale (never A+, D±, which Notre Dame's import read as ungraded). | Claude (DGS item 12) | — |

The saved file adds the date the check ran against and every row's verdict as the page showed it (`stamp`, between `savedAt` and `student`; loading reads `student` alone, so older builds load it). `npm run reproduce -- <file>` finds the snapshot commit with the same fingerprint (or the same content without an optional tab that failed to load), re-runs the check on the saved date and lists every row that reads differently; with no matching snapshot it says so and uses the first snapshot after the rules' date. Not a security measure: nothing is verified with it. | Claude (DGS item 8) | — |

It halves the false rows (L2–L5 73 → 28; L6 38 → 14) and reads a turned page 15–16 points better, but its flag recall falls 64.6 → 21.4 % — its character probabilities stay high on rows it reads wrong, so the ⚠ a student relies on would need a new confidence rule — and term cells 99.3 → 95.5 %. The adapter's figures were each measured on L0 or L1, never on a scored level: a detected box × 0.75 = the Tesseract line height (median ratio 1.33 over 1687 boxes), an unspaced line cut at a 3-pitch gap, NFKC on every word, and page 1's orientation trial in the shipped shape (floor 90, margin 5) plus one rule the runtime forces — it turns tall crops a quarter, so a page on its side reads at its upright confidence and a reading whose lines run down the page never stands (all 124 L6 documents turned right, no upright one). Numbers, method and re-run commands: docs/OCR-BENCHMARK.md "Engine gate". The runner stays for a re-run on new parser code or a new model (PP-OCRv6_small is the research's accuracy pick); any engine past the 7.5 MB `public/ocr/` cap (this one ≈ 20.7 MB with onnxruntime-web's 14.24 MB WebAssembly) still needs the DGS. | Claude Code (the gate the DGS set in the program row of 2026-10-09) | — |

A reload reopens in the mode (the stored copy is found first); Exit asks first, naming what is discarded (courses, milestone dates, other changes, the simulated semester), then puts the real record back from memory without re-saving it; with no real record on the device the opening dialog returns, as after Reset. The engine is untouched: `audit(student, rules, today)` is called with the simulated date. `src/ui/simulation.ts`, `tests/simulation.test.ts`. | Claude (design D1/D2 of the 2026-10-09 plan; DGS 2026-10-09, simulation mode) | — |

The audit, the Grad Admin request's count, the standing chip ("simulated current semester: Spring 2028"), the full-time-terms list, the new course's default year and the prior-rules check read it. The transcript's empty semesters and unregistered gaps (questions about the transcript), the rules' date line, the loading card, the print header's "printed on" and a saved file's `savedAt` keep the real date. | Claude (design D5; DGS 2026-10-09, simulation mode) | — |

The evidence, same parser: a flat 300 on the ladder's 150–200-dpi sources was a wash at L2 (medium set, 62 seeds, 151 pages: row accuracy 54.1 → 53.7 %, rows found 88.1 → 89.6, false rows 16 → 15) and LOST at L5 (58.1 → 52.2 %, field accuracy 83.8 → 80.7; the Minerva pages 3 → 0, 4 → 0, 4 → 0 rows right — the rows themselves read better at 300, at higher confidence, but the header cell "Cr./C.E.U." came out "Cr./C.E\U." and the parser's header-mapped path lost every grade under it, the step-2.5 brittleness; Alberta 9 → 4) at 1.2 × the time; on 300-dpi sources (L0 rendered at 300 dpi, 62 seeds) 300 beat 216 clearly: exact 36 → 40 of 62, row accuracy 66.3 → 70.0 %, rows found 89.3 → 92.2 %, precision 98.0 → 99.2 %, false rows 15 → 9 (Duke's key 6 → 2, Waterloo's 1 → 0; the IITB request form and Tokyo's grading page 0 → 1 each), field accuracy 87.8 → 88.4 %, 1.99 → 2.35 s/page (1.18 ×). So a page is never rendered past its scan's resolution unless that is under 216, and between 216 and 300 it is read 1:1 (the band between the two measured points; no resampling). | 2026-10-09 | (OCR step 11, the confidence rule and the floor — which figure does a line built from word boxes carry, and where does `OCR_CONFIDENCE_FLOOR` sit? The engine gives a confidence per word and per line; the plan asked for the least word confidence, an A/B against the engine's line figure on the bench's flag precision / recall, and the floor re-calibrated from the numbers — it had never been measured against a scan.) | `OCR_LINE_CONFIDENCE = 'min-word'`: an emitted line is as sure as its least sure word (the runs that made the line come back from `layout.ts` `pageLayout().lineRuns`; a line with no word of its own — only the institution line a watermark would contribute, which the engine never reads — gets 0, flagged, never trusted). The floor STAYS 80. Measured: `--quick` (6 seeds) flag precision / recall at the floor, min-word vs the engine's line figure: L0 44.4 / 80.0 % vs 80.0 / 40.0 %, L2 41.2 / 63.6 % vs 66.7 / 18.2 %, L5 70.6 / 85.7 % vs 85.7 / 42.9 %. The calibration (`bench-out/ocr-step11-calibration.json`: 32 positive documents — 8 external seeds at L1 / L2 / L3 / L5 from the clean before ladder's degraded PDFs, 263 rows, 189 of them wrong in at least one of the five cells — and 26 negatives at L2): with min-word, precision is 72–75 % at EVERY floor from 70 to 88 and recall equals the share of rows flagged (floor 70: 49 % recall for 47 % flagged; 80: 60 / 60; 85: 71 / 70; 90: 82 / 82) — the share of wrong rows, 72 %, is the precision, so the floor is a volume knob with no knee, and nothing in the data names a better number than the 80 the app has used; the engine-line figure at 80 flags 18 % of rows and catches 21 % of the wrong ones (81 % precision), at 90 50 % / 53 % — the same curve, shifted. min-word is kept because at the floor the app has it catches three times the wrong rows (the flag exists so the student checks the row against the paper; a miss is silent), its meaning is a guarantee per word, and the two rules are equal at matched volume. What the numbers also say, for step 2.5: the engine is confidently wrong on digits — the pinned pages read "3.0" as "30" at 93 and "3.00" as "300" at 88 while right rows sit at 46–67 — so no floor separates right rows from wrong ones; a cell-level check (`oddCredits` is the first) is the route. The floor's comment in `src/transcript/external.ts` records this. | Claude Code (auto-approved program; DGS 2026-10-09) | — |

One rule of its own, measured on the pinned pages: words of one engine line whose gap is at most 0.55 of the line's height (`WORD_SPACE_SHARE`; a word space measures 0.2–0.5 of it, a cell gap 0.75 or more, nothing between) are ONE phrase run — as pdfjs gives the layout "College of Science" as one item; per-word runs made "Science", printed in two columns and a header line twelve times at four x positions, pass for a watermark tile and vanish from "Science of Programming". A second tier, later the same day: the app's own scan fixture sets its course rows in a MONOSPACE face, where a word space is a whole character cell — 0.8–0.9 of the line height once the bearings are added — so the 0.55 share cut "Operating Systems" into two cells (a phone photo's perspective stretches a proportional title's spaces the same way: 0.76–0.96 on the pinned L5 page); two TITLE WORDS — letters only, two of them at least, three when all capitals, so "TR" / "CR" / "NG" / "IP" / a one-letter grade never join a title — up to 1.1 of the line height apart are one phrase (`TITLE_WORD_SPACE_SHARE`, `titleWord`; the narrowest column gap on the pinned pages is 1.14). Seeds: the generator's twelve PDFs and the scan fixture (ground truth = the exact path's own reading of the same PDF, so L0 must reproduce it; a generator PDF that reads no rows is a negative), every `pdf` entry of `sources.json` kept OUTSIDE the repo and rebuilt only by a download verified against the pinned SHA-256 and byte size (a dead link or a changed file is logged and skipped, never guessed — on 2026-10-09 four links were dead and Rice's key had changed upstream, so 49 of 54 seed the bench), line-list fixtures rendered to pages in three skins as `synthetic-render` (a layout no registrar printed: reported separately, regressions only), and private seeds through `--seeds-dir`, named `private-NN` in every output, one aggregate line in `results.md`, nothing about them in a committed file. Ladder L0–L7 in Pillow + numpy, every level seeded and every drawn value in a manifest; the seal and the watermark are DRAWN (ring, star, invented wording), never a real seal or logo; each level is re-wrapped as an image-only PDF (the bench's own writer — Pillow's would re-encode every page as JPEG q75) so the app's real path runs, and L7 — a PDF with a poor text layer — takes the exact path, as the app does. Scoring: the one scorer for rows and header fields plus line CER (Levenshtein, per page), flag precision/recall (`lowConfidence` rows against rows actually wrong) and seconds per page; "row accuracy" = rows with all five cells right / expected rows, beside the replay's "rows found". A `text` reference row (the exact path on the source) shows the parser's own ceiling. Beneath it: rows are aligned the way the app matches a course to a sheet row, by `normalizeCourseId` plus the year when both sides have one (a retake pairs with its own year), then by id alone among the unmatched, each expected row to at most one parsed row in document order; recall = matched / expected rows and precision = matched / parsed rows, both over POSITIVE fixtures; cell accuracy (title, credits, grade, term, level) only over the matched pairs, so a wrong id costs one missing and one extra row and a wrong grade costs one grade cell; a `negative` fixture (key, legend, form) scores rows only — every parsed row is a false positive, reported separately as "false rows on negatives", never folded into precision. Can any of them be recognized by the imported transcripts, and then allow students to make changes?” Then: “I want Option 1”, with two redacted insideND transcripts — a student who moved into the Ph.D. after one semester of the MSCSE, and a 4+1 B.S./M.S. student who also entered the Ph.D. | **Option 1.** The opening dialog keeps the alpha notice and the program. The earlier-degrees questions move to the Transcripts card, asked on the page until answered, with a Next-steps item and the standing card’s line pointing there. Imports fill in what a transcript can tell, marked “Read from your transcripts: …” under the question — never over a part already there, never once an answer exists; the student’s change drops the mark; the import’s toast says the earlier degrees were partly filled in. What the two transcripts showed a Notre Dame transcript can tell: whether the bachelor’s is Notre Dame’s (undergraduate terms) and its department (the LAST undergraduate major — a first-year “Computer Science” that ended in another department reads as that department); a graduate program in another Notre Dame department (non-CSE graduate terms before the CSE ones). NOT read: no undergraduate terms as “another university” — the transcript does not say which levels it covers, so a print limited to the graduate level would look the same (never guess). What it cannot: the MSCSE (regular or 4+1), a move from the MSCSE into the Ph.D. (the first transcript’s graduate terms carry one major throughout and no program or student type), whether an earlier program was finished, and any degree awarded (none is printed). A previous transcript from another university says a bachelor’s there, or a graduate degree there — finished when it prints a conferral, at the bachelor’s university when that transcript is on file. Until the answer is complete the record behaves exactly as an unanswered one did: the partial answer (`backgroundDraft`) is never read by the engine or the inferences. Supersedes, for the earlier degrees, 2026-09-22 “ask everything in the opening dialog” and 2026-09-23 “force the selections in each family”; the program is still forced. A read value stays if its import is removed. The adversarial review of the change (same night) added: the questions, once a student starts answering on the page, stay open — saved as they go — until “Done”, so the completing click keeps its focus and a follow-up it reveals (the 4+1 admission term, the transfer term) is seen; “Done” and the Change dialog’s “Save” are the student’s check, after which nothing is marked as read; a saved answer missing a follow-up asked since it was given goes back to a draft when the dialog closes (the dialog used to force it); a program change keeps only the parts that still apply and undoes what the old answer settled; an import-time inference never stands against the partial answer (a Notre Dame master’s is not “the MSCSE held” once the student says another department; a “No” keeps prior study at none; a “No” to the 4+1 withdraws an inferred 4+1); a reading’s “finished” / “same university” fill only where the answer asks them about that degree; the emails say “Prior graduate study: not answered yet” instead of “No prior graduate degree”; every pointer to the removed standing controls now names the earlier-degrees questions in the Transcripts card; only a positive reading is evidence — a major cell that is a fragment of a CSE major (“Computer”), a placeholder or a standing value reads nothing, and a College / Major row with a cell missing is not read; “same university” is read only against a single earlier undergraduate transcript (not a master’s transcript’s own undergraduate rows); two transcripts that disagree about a part neither answered by the student clear it for the student to answer, and the toast says so; and a row stays while its preview is open (and a graduate row while its courses are on file), so an answer given meanwhile cannot hide a transcript or its Remove. | **DGS** | §3.5, §4.5, §5.2 |

Show the entries only when they are in progress.”) When can the OCE start? | CSE §4.5 states only the coursework precondition (“All coursework for the Ph.D. must be completed (or in progress the same semester) before the candidacy exam can be taken”); the qualifier precondition is the DGS’s rule here. The OCE can be scheduled once the qualifier umbrella is complete. It can also be scheduled once the research component is passed and every other open qualifier part (core areas, specialization, §4.2’s nine Notre Dame credits) is In progress on courses of this semester or earlier, i.e. expected complete at the end of this semester. Until then the OCE row and the admission-to-candidacy card read Not started, keeping their semester-8 deadline chip. Overdue still reads Overdue, and a missing OCE parameter still cannot be evaluated. Milestones (Ph.D.): the OCE date and the qualifier completion form appear once the OCE can be scheduled (or the qualifier is complete). Admission and defense still appear after the OCE is dated, and the final dissertation submission after the defense. The MSCSE candidacy application appears once due. A field with a date on file is always shown. | Engine + display. | DGS |

(1) `phd.candidacy`, “Oral Candidacy Exam (OCE) passed” (§4.5), is the EXAM only: the eighth-semester deadline, the coursework condition (regular credits complete or in progress — 2026-09-12 seventh), the probation note for a late or missing exam (Academic Code §6.2.8); it no longer weighs the GPA or names the RCR training and the form, and once passed it notes that admission is a separate step. (2) `phd.candidacyAdmission`, “Admitted to doctoral candidacy” (Academic Code §6.2.9; DGS Handbook §3.22.3), lists its conditions as facts — the OCE passed, four consecutive full-time semesters (the residency row’s count), the coursework COMPLETE (the same regular-credit measure, graded), the cumulative GPA against `gpa_min` (§2.2), the RCR training — and notes that CSE’s written exam is the dissertation proposal (§4.5) and CSE has no language requirement (§5.3). When every condition is met it says to apply through the Grad Admin, the processing request asks the Grad Admin to initiate the Application for Admission to Doctoral Candidacy, and the advisor summary lists it. Deadline: the end of the eighth semester (“Students must be admitted to degree candidacy by the end of their eighth semester or risk the loss of Graduate School funding”, DGS Handbook §3.22.3; the Graduate School “may” place the student on probation, Academic Code §5.7.3), moved by leave, accommodation and the Spring 2020 cohort’s extra semester like the OCE’s (Item 15, 2026-10-03), from the same entry term — so a transfer from the MSCSE keeps the MSCSE start and a finished Notre Dame MSCSE does not count, as for the OCE (one entry term counts both eighth semesters — confirmed by the DGS the same day). For the Spring 2020 cohort the row names only the funding risk: Appendix A.4 replaces §5.7.3’s admission trigger with the exam by the ninth semester. The Graduate School’s 8 and 4 are code constants (not the sheet’s `candidacy_deadline_semester` / `phd_residency_semesters`, which are the department’s). A new milestone date, “Admitted to doctoral candidacy by the Graduate School”, appears in the Milestones card once the OCE is dated; a dated admission is the Graduate School’s decision and reads Met (after the eighth semester: the DGS confirms the standing; before the OCE date, or with the GPA below 3.0: to the DGS). The eight-year row now needs the admission too (“Admission to candidacy is a prerequisite to receiving a doctoral degree”, §6.2.9). If the course does not appear in the spreadsheet at all, the student needs to use the text-copy button to send it to the DGS so that DGS can review and enter the data to the spreadsheet. After then, the student should revisit the degree checking app that reads the latest data. Then, they should either send a follow-up request to DGS or send the processing request to the grad admin if anything needs to be processed and recorded.” | Implemented as stated. A course’s standing with the DGS is one of four: (1) NOT IN THE SHEET — counted provisionally, “waiting for the DGS — would count … once the DGS has added it to the course rules; not in the course rules yet — send the review request so the DGS can enter it”; no tick can settle it (until now the non-CSE box did, for an unlisted non-CSE course — no longer); (2) `yes` — counts outright, no tick: for a transfer course the line reads “counts toward regular courses (3 cr); approved by the DGS in the course rules — send the Grad Admin the processing request to have it recorded (§5.2)” (revising 2026-09-07’s “provisional until processed”), and the transfer row names those courses for the processing request; (3) `no` — not counted; (4) `dgs_approval` / `adgs_approval` — case by case: the row carries its own tick, “The DGS approved this course for me” (`CourseEntry.dgsApproved`), shown only on such a course; ticked, the course counts, leaves the review request and reaches the processing request as “approved by the DGS for my case”. The three record-level boxes (below the 60000 level, non-CSE, transfer) are gone from the milestones card; a saved file from before this maps each ticked box onto every course it covered and clears it (`migrateApprovalBoxes`, state.ts) — the engine honours the mark only on a case-by-case course, so nothing counts that did not. The review card states the process once: “A course that is not in the course rules yet goes to the DGS through this request; the DGS enters it — yes, no, or case by case — and this page reads the updated rules the next time you open it. A course marked case by case needs the DGS’s answer for you: send this request, then tick the box next to the course once it is approved.” Its lines say which kind each course is (“not in the course rules yet — the DGS enters it” / “listed as case by case — needs the DGS’s approval for you (§5.2)”); the Next steps split the review request the same way and add “When the DGS answers, come back to this page — it reads the latest course rules — and tick the box next to each course approved for you; then send the processing request”. The Approvals row lists a `yes` transfer whose §4.4.1 core area is still unrecorded under the DGS (its reason “the DGS has still to record its core-knowledge area”), since the review request still asks about it. The plan-of-study, qualifier-extension and prior-rules boxes stay record-level. | 2026-09-18 | Interface review R1: what happens to a number outside the range its own box advertises. | The `min`/`max` attributes were decorative — a GPA of 35 stored and read “Cumulative GPA 35.00 meets the 3.0 minimum” under a green Met pill; a course at 999 credits (box max 15) was accepted. Now: one range table (`src/engine/ranges.ts`) read by the form, the save-file loader, the transcript import and the engine. A value outside it is REFUSED on commit — the typed text stays in its box, marked `aria-invalid` with a message beside it and announced in the live region, and never reaches `Student` or localStorage. Three further readings, taken by Claude within the DGS's “Fix, and give an error for an out-of-bound GPA”: (a) a stored GPA off the 0.00–4.00 scale makes §2.2 `cannot_evaluate`, not `met` and not `unmet` — it is no answer, not a low one; (b) a GPA a SAVED FILE carries is dropped and reported rather than refusing the whole file, because `loadLocal()` runs the same code and throwing there would silently discard the record already on the device; (c) a cumulative GPA a transcript misreads off the scale is never offered in the preview, and the preview's warnings say so. The ranges are the shape of the number (ND's 4.00 scale, a term's year), not policy: every threshold, `gpa_min` included, still comes from the sheet. Two more readings came out of the adversarial pass over the finished change: (d) EVERY row that compares the GPA to §2.2's minimum reads it through `usableGpa()`, so an off-scale figure gates nothing and is quoted nowhere — one report may not say both “cannot be checked” and “yours is -2.00”; (e) `gpa: null` in a record means NOT ENTERED, not a refusal (it used to pass every `!== undefined` test downstream and throw in the §4.5 candidacy gate, blanking the page on load). STILL DECORATIVE and left for the DGS to bound: the external-transcript preview's per-row credits (box max 30) and year (box min 1970) boxes, and course credits / term years inside a saved file — each needs an upper bound only the DGS can set. | **DGS** (review of 2026-09-18) + Claude (a–e) | §2.2 |

The iframe src names the host pages (`?course_rules_url=…` on the self-check page, `?self_check_url=…` on the course-rules page); `siblingLink` then targets `_top` with that URL, else the relative sibling file. http(s) only. README documents the embed. | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-16 (fifth) | Putting the pages inside a page on ND's WordPress site (`sites.nd.edu`), which strips `<iframe>`/`<script>` from post content. | An explicit `?embed=1` (or `#embed`) renders either page as a guest: no ND eyebrow, the `<h1>` kept for screen readers only, “Who to contact” moved to the end, no outer margins, and an “Open the full page ↗” link that leaves the frame (`target="_top"`, as do all links that would otherwise load a whole page inside it). Never sniffed from `window.top` — an explicit parameter can be linked and tested. **courses.html** also broadcasts its height to the parent so the frame grows and shrinks with the content; **index.html** deliberately does not — its consent dialog, toasts and sticky score need a frame with a viewport of its own, so it keeps a fixed height and its own scrollbar, and it warns the student that an embedded page's saved work lives in the frame's storage (Safari blocks it outright). `postMessage` targetOrigin is an allowlist of three ND hosts — `https://sites.nd.edu`, `https://cse.nd.edu`, `https://www.nd.edu` — never `'*'`; the page listens for no incoming message at all. Height cap 100 000 px, not the 20 000 first proposed: 117 courses measure 42 290 px at a 700 px column, and the parent trusts the number, so a lower cap would hide real rows in a frame that cannot scroll. The WordPress half is `docs/wordpress-footer-snippet.html`, pasted into the “Head, Footer and Post Injections” plugin; README § “Embedding these pages in a WordPress page” is the DGS's instructions. `tests/embed.test.ts` plus the “course rules list” e2e leg pin it, including that a parent outside the allowlist receives nothing. | **DGS** | — |

The current behaviour is correct and stays: `ndRegular`/`ndResearch` are gated on `origin === 'nd'`, which is exactly "taken while enrolled in this program". No code change to the counting; the nine-credit row now SAYS so when the student actually has such coursework, so a shortfall does not read as a data-entry problem they could fix. Neither changes what counts. Cross-origin ids still do not de-duplicate (2026-08-31 — ids may legitimately collide across universities), but nobody sits one course at two institutions in a term, so it is almost always one course entered twice; a future-dated final grade is still counted, since the app takes the student's word for their own record, but a graded semester that has not happened yet is usually a typo. Both are warnings in `classify()`; `classify` now takes `today` (optional) for the second. | **DGS** (2026-09-13) | — |

A red-team pass found the card asking for approval on a D in a listed 40000-level course with no core area, and on a C- whose core area the Courses tab already gives. | DGS: **no** — stop asking. A passed grade below C earns no credit (Academic Code §4.3, decision of 2026-09-12) and cannot clear §4.4.2's B floor either, so the only thing a ruling could still add is a §4.4.1 core area — and only for a Ph.D. student, on a course the sheet does not list, whose title names an area. `coursesNeedingDgsReview` skips the rest. (An F never reached the request anyway, but only because the failed-grade branch returns before `approvalPending` is set — the same intent, by accident of code order rather than by a rule.) | **DGS** (2026-09-13) | §4.3 (Academic Code), §4.4.1, §4.4.2 |
| 2026-09-13 | How long is a §4.4 qualifier extension? The handbook says only "the DGS may extend the deadline on a case-by-case basis", and the app carried it as a bare boolean that forgave lateness with no time bound at all — a research qualifier five years past its deadline still read "due soon" against the original date, forever. | DGS: **one additional semester at a time.** `deadlineStatus` now takes the extended deadline itself (the end of the term after the original — `term.ts:endOfNextSemester`) instead of a "forgive it" flag, so the extension runs out on schedule, a pass inside it is met and says how late it was, and a pass past it goes back to needing the DGS. A further extension is the DGS's to grant. §4.5 candidacy is untouched: the handbook gives the DGS no extension power there (confirmed against §4.4's text and the 2026-08-31 deadline-arithmetic row), so a late candidacy pass still goes to the DGS. Tests: tests/scenarios/phd-qualifier-extension-runs-out.json, tests/engine-units.test.ts ('an extension is one additional semester'). | **DGS** (2026-09-13) | §4.4, §4.4.3, §4.5 |

Raised by the same review: the Grad School Academic Code §4.3 ("Grades of C- and D … will not be accepted for completion of graduate degree requirements, specific required coursework, and/or total credit hours") is stricter than the 2026-08-31 default ("any non-failing grade earns credit"). | DGS: tighten it — no C-/D counts toward a credit-hour requirement. Core knowledge (§4.4.1) is untouched: it asks only whether the course was passed. AS BUILT: `grades.ts`'s new `passesCreditFloor()` (meets a C floor, or still in progress — a final grade below C never fills a pool or a cap). Wired into `allocate.ts`'s counting loop and its "ineligible courses" loop, so a below-floor course never reaches `sums.regular` / `sums.total` / `ndRegular` / `ndResearch`, and into the §3.5 bachelor's-share selection and §4.2's two-credit Research Seminar row (both are credit-hour requirements too). `ctx.classified` — what §4.4.1's `coreRows()` and §4.4.2's `categoriesRow()` read — is unchanged, so core knowledge and the §4.4.2 B floor (already stricter) are unaffected. Supersedes the 2026-08-31 default for CREDIT only; that default still decides what counts as passed. Test: phd-grade-below-c-excluded-from-credit.json. | **DGS** (2026-09-12) | Academic Code §4.3; §3.2, §4.2 |

The Ph.D. side already blocked the first; neither existed for the MSCSE. | DGS: yes to both fixes. AS BUILT (`allocate.ts`): the existing §5.2 "a master's project or thesis does not transfer" exclusion (`isProject`, previously gated on `student.program === 'phd'`) now applies to both programs; a NEW guard blocks a 4+1's PRE-BACHELOR'S CSE 68901/68902 registration from landing in the MSCSE's project pool by the same route (a project/thesis credit must be earned while enrolled in the MSCSE — never transferred in, never picked up as an undergraduate elective); and §3.5's `undergrad()` selection (which chooses which courses share credit with the bachelor's degree) now requires `courseType === 'regular'` — an unlisted course keeps the benefit of the doubt, as elsewhere in this file. Tests: mscse-thesis-does-not-transfer.json, mscse-4plus1-research-course-not-shared.json. | **DGS** (2026-09-12) | §3.4, §3.5, §5.2 |

New `src/transcript/campus.ts`: sixteen multi-campus systems, each campus with its full name (the ExternalCourses key) and the aliases a transcript may print (city, acronym, town — “La Jolla”, “UCSD”); `resolveCampus` reads the campus from the name, then from the header (first 40 lines — a city deep in the record may be another school's address). The preview shows a required “Which University of California campus?” select (`ext.preview.campus`); Add refuses until chosen; the choice becomes the university name. The Ph.D. is untouched: §3.2 is the master's section and a Ph.D. student's thesis-direction credits are research credits. Tests: tests/scenarios/mscse-thesis-direction.json. (2) A COURSE ENTERED WITH 0 CREDITS said only “not counted”, with no reason, no warning and nothing pointing at the credits field — and a blank credit-hours column is an ordinary transcript-parse artifact. DGS: “allow 0 credits for courses with grades, but show a warning message and double-check with the students [when] they attempt to add 0-credit courses.” The row is kept and counts nothing, which is what 0 credits means; its line now reads “not counted — entered with 0 credits; check the credit hours on your transcript”, a warning names the course, and both the manual form and the transcript preview ask before adding one (`confirmDialog` in copy-dialog.ts, the safe answer focused, Escape cancels). Tests: tests/scenarios/mscse-zero-credit-course.json, drive-app.mjs. (3) THE MSCSE HAD NO §5.2 TRANSFER ROW. The cap was enforced — a fourth transferred course read “over the transfer-credit cap” — but nothing said what the cap was, so a student could only find it by exceeding it. The Ph.D.'s own row moved to src/engine/requirements/transfer.ts and both degrees now use it, the master's with `ms_transfer_completed_ms_credits_max` (9) or `transfer_unfinished_ms_credits_max` (6). New id `ms.transfer`. Tests: tests/scenarios/mscse-transfer-cap.json. (4) TWO SENTENCES. A row that is MET no longer carries its “Register for…” advice (`extraDetail` is now added only while the row is unmet) — a finished student was being told to register for the course they had just finished. And a course entered twice in the SAME term — what a hand-typed course plus an import of the same transcript produces — said “the Fall 2025 attempt of this course is the one counted”, naming the term the student is already looking at and identifying neither row; it now reads “entered twice for Fall 2025 — credits count once (§4.4.2), so this duplicate row counts nothing. Remove it if it is not a second registration.” A genuine retake in a later term keeps the old sentence, which was right. | **DGS** (2026-09-11, from the corner-case run) | §3.2, §3.4, §5.2 |

AS BUILT: for an MSCSE student, Notre Dame coursework at the 60000 level and above taken as an undergraduate counts only in the three fall/spring semesters ending with the one the bachelor's degree was awarded in (`semesterNumber(bachelorsAwarded, term) >= -1`); earlier than that it reads “not counted — taken before the second semester of your junior year, which is where §3.5 begins…”. A summer term reports the preceding semester, so the summer between the junior and senior years is inside the window. The award term is what places a course in the student's academic years, so without it nothing can be placed: the line then asks for it rather than guessing. THREE deliberate boundaries: the Ph.D. keeps every term (the Graduate School's answer is the Ph.D.'s rule and names none); 40000-level coursework has no window either, since its allowance is §3.2's and the BS/MSCSE double count names no term; and a December graduate's window is still the three semesters ending at graduation, which for them is the junior fall rather than the junior spring. The window is §3.5's shape, not a tunable, so it is in code with the handbook sentence quoted — say the word and it becomes a Parameters key. Tests: tests/engine-units.test.ts (‘§3.5's window on graduate coursework taken as an undergraduate’). | **DGS** (2026-09-11) | §3.2, §3.5 |

This supersedes decision Q19 for LISTED courses only: a 50000-level course the sheet does not list still counts nothing, since the permission has to come from the DGS. Test: tests/scenarios/phd-below-60000-cap.json. | **DGS** (instruction 2026-09-09) | §4.2, §3.2 |
| 2026-09-09 | Six defects found while checking the two Notre-Dame-degree cases end to end (each reproduced by running the engine before it was fixed). | (1) Every transfer landed in the REGULAR pool, so a prior Notre Dame research or thesis course counted toward §4.2's twenty-four regular credits — which §4.2 excludes in as many words — and a prior 40000-level course never touched the six-credit cap. An earlier Notre Dame course now keeps its own Courses-tab verdict (course_type, level, counts_toward_*) on top of §5.2's cap; courses from other universities are unchanged, since the Courses tab describes Notre Dame's catalogue. (2) "Prior graduate study" was worked out only during a transcript import, so correcting the entry term afterwards left it at "none" and applied a 6-credit cap instead of 24; it now follows the coursework (`derivePriorMs`). (3) A 4+1's senior-year graduate course was read as a prior master's, putting "Prior M.S., not completed" into students' emails — a course dated in or before the bachelor's award term no longer counts as prior graduate study. (4) A hand-typed Notre Dame course dated before the entry term stayed program coursework for good, earning §4.2 credits, §4.3 residence and §4.4.2 specialization; the manual form now re-files it as the import does. (5) A Notre Dame transcript dropped into a "Previous …" slot left the student's IN-PROGRAM courses filed as transfer candidates; that path now re-files by the entry term too. (6) The §5.2 row said "needs DGS review" when every entered course was excluded on its own terms and there was nothing to ask for; it is now "not applicable" with a line saying so. Also: the §5.2 explanatory paragraph is no longer suppressed above a group of prior Notre Dame coursework — §5.2's last sentence covers it, and a student who never left Notre Dame is the most likely to assume their courses simply carry over. | Claude Code (defects; found verifying the DGS's two cases) | §4.2, §4.4.2, §5.2 |
| 2026-09-09 | On an iPhone the schedule cards' course titles ran past the card edge and dragged the whole course-rules page sideways (DGS report, iPhone 14 Pro / Safari). | The one-course-one-line rule those tables carry is a DESK rule: below 600 px the same table is a stack of cards, where there are no columns to line up and a title that cannot wrap simply overflows. `white-space` goes back to normal there. Measured at 393 px: the page's scroll width was 536 px against a 393 px viewport, and is 393 now. The e2e's no-sideways-scrolling check would not have caught it while the sheet's schedule columns were blank, so it now also fails if any schedule cell's right edge passes its card. Two e2e selectors had to be scoped while fixing it: the schedule tables carry the `course-rules` class for styling, so `table.course-rules` had started matching them too, and every row count on the page now says `.all-courses table.course-rules`. | **DGS** (bug report) | — |
| 2026-09-09 | The live ExternalCourses tab had eight duplicated (university, course) pairs — sixteen rows, three copies of some — because a student may take the same course several times (a master's project, thesis credits) and the review request emitted a paste-ready row per ATTEMPT. The last row wins, so the earlier rulings were shadowed. | The copied request now emits one row per COURSE, keyed the way the sheet itself matches (`normalizeUniversity` + `normalizeCourseId`, so "CS-59800" and "CS 59800" at one university are one course). The course DETAILS below still list every attempt — that is the evidence the DGS weighs, and the repetition stays visible there — and the tables say so when a repeat was folded away. Nothing changes in the student's own coursework list, where each attempt still counts its own credits. | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-09 | Whether a course from another university transfers can depend on the degree: the DGS split `transferable` into `transferable_PhD` and `transferable_MSCSE`, and added a fourth value, `adgs_approval`. | The engine resolves the ruling ONCE, in `classify()`, against the student's own program, and puts it on `ClassifiedCourse.transferable` — so nothing downstream has to know which column to read, and a Ph.D. ruling can never be applied to an MSCSE student. `dgs_approval` and `adgs_approval` are one case for now, on the DGS's instruction, and both are told to the student as "needs DGS approval" (the §5.2 card lists them under "Needs DGS approval", the review request says "transfer needs DGS approval (§5.2)"). The two words are kept apart in the type rather than collapsed at parse time, so the day the MSCSE message should name the ADGS instead, the sheet's own word is still there to key on. A sheet still carrying the single `transferable` column fills both programs, so nothing breaks mid-rename. | **DGS** | §5.2 |
| 2026-09-09 | The DGS's `notes` were readable by students, through a Notes column and a per-row disclosure on the course-rules page. | Gone from the page: no column, no button, no legend entry, and the notes no longer feed the hover text either. | 2026-09-09 | The course-rules filters read as a ragged wrap, and the free-text search box was taller than the selects beside it. | One grid of equal cells, `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(180px, 1fr))`, with every control the same 36 px box; the view picker spans the row above, and the two switches and Clear share a line below. | 2026-09-09 | `offered_semester` named the semester the two schedule columns were written for. The DGS saw that one stamp could serve more generally — "as of which semester is the spreadsheet up-to-date" — and asked whether the schedule could still be driven from it. | Yes, and the logic did not change: if the sheet as a whole is current for FA26 then `offered_now` describes FA26 and `offered_next` describes SP27, which is exactly what the shift already assumed. The key is `current_semester`; `offered_semester` is still read, so a sheet that has not been renamed keeps working, and `current_semester` wins where both appear. The generalisation does cost something, and the docs say so: the stamp now means "the DGS says the sheet is current as of X" rather than "the schedule columns were written for X", so moving it forward for an unrelated edit, without revisiting those two columns, republishes an old schedule under a new semester's name — the one thing the key exists to prevent. The three cells are one edit. | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-09 | The schedule cards name their semesters from today's date while `offered_now` / `offered_next` are static, so a DGS who did not update them the week a semester turned over would have the page show last semester's courses under this semester's name (DGS's own concern). | The Parameters tab gains `offered_semester`, a display-only key naming the semester `offered_now` was written for, written as the code the pages already print — `FA26`, `SP27`, fall or spring only (DGS 2026-09-09: "I'm afraid people will make mistakes in filling the values", and four characters leave less room for one than "Fall 2026"; case, a space or a hyphen and a four-digit year are all forgiven, the long form still reads, a summer code is refused because a schedule cannot be dated to one, and anything else is reported). The parameter is read once during assembly so that its diagnostic reaches the self-check page's diagnostics card and `npm run sync-sheet` — the typed accessors only report when something asks, and only the course-rules page asks, which shows no diagnostics. `effective_term` takes the code too, so the sheet has one way of writing a semester. The page compares it with today: the same semester → both columns as recorded; the semester BEFORE → what was recorded as "next" IS this semester, so it moves across and "next" reads "Not released yet."; anything else, including a missing value → both cards say "Not released yet." and a line says why. This is the project's never-guess rule applied to a schedule: a late update makes the page show LESS than it could, never something false. Summer counts as the coming fall, since schedules are kept for fall and spring. The same shift drives the table filter, so the cards and the table can never disagree. `src/ui/schedule-terms.ts`, with a test matrix over the rollover, the summer and the missing value. | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-09 | The schedule columns needed to be visible, not only filterable, and the overview cards wrapped every course title onto two lines. | Two cards, "Offered this semester — Fall 2026" and "Offered next semester — Spring 2027", above the qualifier overview, since the schedule matters to MSCSE and Ph.D. students alike. Each lists its courses with every attribute the main table carries EXCEPT the three the DGS excluded — "Typically offered" (a pattern from past years, which these cards supersede), "DGS reviewed" (the * beside a course id says it) and the notes. A semester with no `yes` and no `no` anywhere reads "Not released yet." rather than showing an empty list, which would say "nothing runs". Card widths are content-driven now: `.ov-grid` is a flex row whose cards size to their longest course, so one course sits on one line wherever there is room; the schedule cards take the whole row until a window can fit both seven-column tables. On a phone the text wraps again, since the cards are full width there. | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-09 | The Courses tab gained `offered_now` and `offered_next` (`yes` / `no`), and the DGS wants the course-rules page to show which courses are running this semester and next — without adding two columns to the table. | One filter, "On the schedule", with "Offered this semester (Fall 2026)" and "Offered next semester (Spring 2027)" — the semesters are named from today's date at Notre Dame, and "next" is the next FALL or SPRING, since summer is not a graduate teaching term. Only a `yes` puts a course in the list: a blank cell means the sheet has not said, and is never read as "will not run". The control and its legend entry appear only once some course carries a value, so a page whose sheet is not filled in never shows a filter that could only return nothing. The filter is in the shared link like every other. `RuleCourse.offeredNow` / `.offeredNext`; nothing outside the course-rules page reads them, and the self-check is untouched. | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-09 | The page stopped at 1240 px and centred, so a wide monitor showed the cards at a fixed width with empty margins either side. | `#app` has no width cap: the page fills the window at any width, with its 20 px padding. The two columns grow together — the inputs column is `minmax(480px, 30%)` rather than a fixed 480 px, so it no longer stays put while the report column takes every extra pixel. Paragraphs inside a card keep a 100-character reading measure, so the tables, the course lists and the requirement rows use the room while a sentence does not run the width of the glass; that is the one deliberate limit, and it is easy to lift. The tablet and phone breakpoints (1120 px, 900 px, 600 px) are untouched, and the e2e now fails if either page stops filling a 2200 px window or if the inputs column stays at its minimum. | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-09 | The prior Ph.D. transcript upload had no "Bachelor's degree awarded" field, and on the previews that did have one the year box cut the last digit off under the number spinner. | The field is on EVERY graduate-slot preview now, Master's and Ph.D. alike: the award term is what decides which of a transcript's courses were taken with graduate student status (§5.2), so a prior Ph.D. record needs it as much as a Master's one. The bachelor's slot still has none — that transcript is the degree, and its conferral date is read from it. The year box was 58 px with a 13 px font, which fits four digits only if the spinner is not there; it is 66 px in the table's year column and 88 px in the bachelor's field, which is not in a column and can breathe. The e2e measures it. | **DGS** | §5.2 |
| 2026-09-08 | Johns Hopkins' unofficial transcript shows the university's name only in the letterhead IMAGE, so the parser left the institution blank and the import was unusable. | The text does carry the acronym — "JHU Degree and Date Conferred" — so `guessUniversity` gained a LAST-RESORT table, `NAME_ONLY_IN_IMAGE` in src/transcript/external.ts, mapping an acronym to the school's real name. It runs only after every attempt to read a name from the text has failed, so a transcript that spells its name out always wins, and the pattern is case-sensitive so a lower-case look-alike (a surname, a web address) cannot rename a transcript. The recorded name is "Johns Hopkins University", spelled as the university spells it; the ExternalCourses tab matches case-insensitively, so a row written in capitals still matches. Its dotted course code ("EN.601.433" — division, department, course) is read too, both as one token and in the two-cell layout; both dots are required, so "VOL.12" and "MAY.2025" are still not course codes. UC San Diego (DGS 2026-09-09) needed the same entry plus two more fixes: a page tiled with a "UNIVERSITY OF CALIFORNIA, SAN DIEGO" watermark was leaving a fragment ("UNIVERSITY") as the name, so a cell that is only a generic institution word, or that repeats one, is no longer a candidate; and the acronym is now tried BEFORE the weak "… College" pass, because that pass was picking up the school named in the transcript's "DEGREES AWARDED BY OTHER INSTITUTIONS" block. A name worked out from an acronym is marked as a guess: the preview pre-fills it but leaves the box editable and says where it came from, unlike a name printed as text, which stays locked. Reviewing the Johns Hopkins work also turned up three parser faults it would have hit: a degree heading printed on a line of its own ("JHU Degree and Date Conferred", values below) did not open the degree block, so a completed Master's read as not completed and the §5.2 cap fell from 24 to 6; a section number between the code and the title was read as the credits; and a January Intersession or a Winter quarter inherited the previous header's season, dating a course months late with the term locked against correction. Tests: tests/external-transcript.test.ts ("a university whose name is printed only as an image", "a transcript whose watermark looks like its name"). | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-08 | The ExternalCourses tab's `transferable` column held only `yes` and `no`, so a course the DGS is willing to transfer FOR SOME STUDENTS — one outside the usual CSE ground that serves a particular dissertation — had no honest value: `yes` pre-approved it for everyone and `no` shut it out. | A third value, `dgs_approval`, the same word the Courses tab uses. It is a decision about the COURSE and an open question about the STUDENT, so the engine treats it as a transfer candidate and says why: every surface names the open decision and stops there (DGS 2026-09-08, twice: first "that explanation can be shown only once in one place", then "the student doesn't need to know" — whether the course serves the student's research is settled between the advisor and the DGS). The review request says "transferability decided case by case (§5.2)", the sign-off row "transfer — decided case by case by the DGS (§5.2)", the §5.2 card lists the courses under "Decided case by case by the DGS", separate from "Pre-approved" and "Not yet reviewed", and the coursework line says only what it says for any candidate; the review request keeps it and tells the student what to write; the Grad Admin's processing request ignores it until the student attests that the approval came through. `yes` is for a course any student may transfer, `dgs_approval` for one where the answer depends on the student, and a blank cell still means nobody has looked. One wording rule came out of reviewing it: the page never calls the course "outside the usual CSE ground", because `satisfies_core_area` is a separate column and such a course may well have a confirmed §4.4.1 core area on the same line. Tests: tests/external-rules.test.ts ("transferable=dgs_approval → a candidate the DGS decides case by case"), tests/review-pending.test.ts (the request keeps it; hand-typed spellings), tests/grad-admin-request.test.ts (not processable until attested). | **DGS** | §5.2 |
| 2026-09-08 | The full §4.4.1 core-area and §4.4.2 group names made the dense parts of the page hard to read — "Data Science and Artificial Intelligence" did not fit the group dropdown beside a course, and a "Counts toward" cell ran to three lines. | Short forms are used in exactly ten places the DGS approved from screenshots. The ten places: the specialization row's "still open" list and its course → group lines, the group picker and its hint, the "Counts toward" chips, the report's "Credits at ND" meter, the previous-transcript preview notes, the coursework group headings, the Transcripts card's "ND Unofficial Transcript" row and the two messages about the wrong file. Everything where the exact wording carries weight keeps the full name: course titles, the requirement cards' own titles, every handbook quote, the glossary that defines the terms, the whole course-rules page, the messages copied for the DGS, the advisor and the Grad Admin, and the masthead and copyright lines (the DGS's call on all three). The substitutions run longest-first so "Computer Architecture" cannot come out "Computer Arch", and the article changes with the name ("a Notre Dame transcript" → "an ND transcript"). | **DGS** | §4.4.1, §4.4.2 |
| 2026-09-08 | The course-rules page carried "Listed under every category" as a sixth specialization category — an overview card and a filter value — which read as a category a course could belong to. | Removed. The table's Specialization column says the same in its own words: "Any one category (student picks)" and "X or Y (student picks one)". | **DGS** | §4.4.2 |

A typo in one code is reported and dropped while the row's other codes keep working. The self-check shows the group picker whenever a course leaves a choice and offers ONLY that course's groups; the course-rules page labels a two-group course "Human Centered Computing or Data Science and Artificial Intelligence (student picks)", matches it under either group in the filter, and lists it in both overview cards. | **DGS** | §4.4.2 |

The requirement's own card keeps the full title, and the link carries it as a tooltip. The e2e fails if any name in the list exceeds 32 characters. | **DGS** | — |

| 2026-09-08 | A course the sheet marks `any` can satisfy any §4.4.2 specialization group, and the dropdown offered a bare list — the student could not tell which choice would actually help. | The engine works out, per flexible course, which groups the student's OTHER courses do not already cover (`groupChoices` on the §4.4.2 row, read off the same matching the requirement uses, so a suggestion is never a group another course is filling). The dropdown sorts its options under "Groups you still need" and "Already covered by another course", and when nothing is chosen a line names the useful groups. Choosing one of them clears the line — no nagging once the student has answered. | **DGS** | §4.4.2 |
| 2026-09-08 | Usability pass over both pages on computer, tablet and phone (DGS request). Fourteen further findings are listed in docs/STATE.md for the DGS to weigh. | **DGS** (request), Claude (which twelve) | — |
| 2026-09-08 | Georgia Tech's UNOFFICIAL transcript prints "Georgia Inst. of Technology"; the abbreviation reached the student's coursework headings, the DGS review request and the Grad Admin processing request. | Institution names are spelled out wherever anyone reads them: `expandInstitutionAbbreviations` (data/external.ts) expands Inst./Univ./Coll./Poly./Intl./Natl./Engr. with or without the full stop, and Tech./Sci./Agri. only with it. The parsed name is stored expanded, so every surface follows; hand-typed names expand too. `normalizeUniversity` applies the same expansion, so a sheet row written either way still matches. Deliberately conservative: "Georgia Tech" and "Virginia Tech" are names in their own right and are untouched, and "St." is left alone since it is Saint in one name and State in another. | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-08 | Georgia Tech prints "ID" for Industrial Design, and the parser threw those courses away because "ID" was on the list of codes that read as an identifier. | "ID" is off the list. A student id is too long to match the course-number pattern anyway. `src/transcript/external.ts`. | **DGS** (bug report) | — |
| 2026-09-08 | Georgia Tech's header prints "Georgia Inst. of Technology", which the institution test did not recognise, so the university was left blank. | The strong-name pattern accepts the abbreviation: `inst(itute)?. of tech`. | **DGS** (bug report) | — |
| 2026-09-08 | A USC Master's that took three years was split as if it were a 4+1, its first year marked undergraduate. | Spanning more than two years is not evidence of an undergraduate degree — a Master's can simply take longer. With no bachelor's on the record, every row keeps the slot's level and the student corrects any row by hand: the app does not guess. `src/transcript/level-prefill.ts`. | **DGS** (bug report) | §5.2 |
| 2026-09-08 | `nd_credits` holds one fixed number, which cannot describe a course worth 2 credits one term and 4 the next, and every quarter-system course had to be entered by hand. | New ExternalCourses column `credit_system` (`quarter` / `semester` / blank). Set on any one row of a university, it applies to every course from that university, listed or not: the credits printed on the student's own transcript are converted (× 2/3), so a varying course converts correctly every time. `nd_credits` keeps its meaning as a FIXED per-course value and still wins where it is set. The converted value is kept EXACTLY (DGS: nine quarter hours are exactly six, not a rounded 7.5 that would cost half a credit against the §5.2 cap); only the printing is shortened to two decimals (`formatCredits`). The course line says "converted from the quarter system" so a student can tell a conversion from the DGS's own figure. | **DGS** (both design choices) | §5.2 |
| 2026-09-08 | A previous-transcript preview cut the "Taken as" dropdown in half and scrolled sideways (DGS screenshot): three rows whose YEAR the parser could not read. | A row got the one-line layout whenever the import was text-layer, even when the parser had missed the credits, the grade or the year — those cells then hold full-size controls, and the one-line form is told not to wrap, so the row overflowed the card. The nowrap rule is also scoped to rows without inputs, as a second guard. | Claude (bug), **DGS** (reported) | — |
| 2026-09-08 | Uploading a Master's transcript reset the "Bachelor's degree awarded" term the student had entered after uploading their undergraduate transcript. | A term the student set by hand now wins: the preview is pre-filled from it, not from the new transcript's conferral date, and says so ("Taken from 'Bachelor's degree awarded' under Your standing — importing this transcript does not change it", naming the transcript's own date so they can correct it deliberately). A term only INFERRED from an earlier transcript still gives way to a later transcript's conferral line, as before. `bachelorsPrefill` in `src/transcript/preview-layout.ts`. The add toast now announces the term only when it actually changed, and calls it "from the conferral date on your transcript" only when it was. | **DGS** | §5.2 |
| 2026-09-07 | The self-check took today's date from the device, so a student abroad, or one with a wrong clock, was audited against the wrong day and could see the wrong semester and deadlines. The DGS asked for "the internet clock for Notre Dame, IN's time zone", shown on the loading page. | Two parts, neither of which contacts a third party. The zone comes from `Intl` with `America/Indiana/Indianapolis` (St. Joseph County, Eastern with US daylight saving), so the calendar is always read as it reads in South Bend. The instant comes from the HTTP `Date` header of one same-origin `HEAD` request to the page's own URL — CLAUDE.md allows same-origin loads of the app's own assets and forbids runtime calls to anyone else, and a public time API would have made the page's printed promise ("nothing is transmitted to the University or to any third party") untrue. `no-store` forces a real request and `Age` is added back if a cache answered; a 2 s timeout, and any failure falls back to the device clock with the loading card saying so. `src/data/clock.ts`; the loading card gains a "Checking today's date at Notre Dame" step; both pages take the resolved date. The two privacy paragraphs now name this second request. | **DGS** (request), Claude (same-origin rather than a public time API) | — |
| 2026-09-07 | The bare "Fall 2026" chip beside "2. Your standing" did not say what it was, and could be read as the entry term. | It reads "current semester: Fall 2026" — it is today's semester, nothing the student entered. | **DGS** | — |
| 2026-09-07 | "Entered the program" named neither the program nor the institution. | The legend names both, from the selected program: "Entered the Ph.D. program at Notre Dame CSE in" / "Entered the M.S. in CSE program at Notre Dame in". Messages that quoted the old field name now say "your entry term". | **DGS** | §3, §4 |
| 2026-09-07 | "Bachelor's degree awarded" was optional. | It is required, whether or not the student holds a prior graduate degree: §5.2 counts a course as transfer credit only when it was taken after the bachelor's degree. | 2026-09-06 | The rules took long to load ("probably because too many tabs are being downloaded — any chance this can be merged into one download?"), and after a first fix (one tab at a time) the load failed outright ("each download takes too much time, so it always failed in 15 seconds") | Measured from the DGS's Mac over the day: any one published-CSV request answers in 0.3–1 s, but about one request in fifteen simply stalls for 10 s or more — whether the tabs are fetched together, two at a time or one after another (fetching three or four at once stalls more often). A single 15-second request per tab therefore failed whenever ANY of the four requests stalled, and one stall used up the whole budget; fetching one at a time did not change that. The fix is not to wait on a stalled request. A later measurement the same day (144 requests, 12 rounds each of four-at-once, two-at-a-time and one-after-another, interleaved) showed the stall rate is the same at any concurrency — 8, 9 and 8 stalls of 48 — with no 429s or other rate-limiting, so (the DGS's question "can we download all tabs at once?") all four tabs are fetched at once, and each attempt is HEDGED: when a request has not answered after 2 s, a second request for the same tab is sent alongside it and the first answer wins (the other is dropped); a request that fails at once (dropped connection, 5xx) is hedged immediately. A whole attempt is abandoned after 6 s (then 10 s, then 14 s for the second and third attempts — the later ones longer so a slow but working connection still gets through) and the tab asked for afresh, up to three attempts; a 4xx or an unpublished sheet is never retried. A stall therefore costs about two seconds, not a failed page. The loading card says when a tab is being asked for again ("Google is slow; asking again" for a hedge; "still nothing; asking again (attempt 2 of 3)" for a fresh attempt) and promises "usually a few seconds; a slow answer is asked for again, up to about 30 s in all". One download is not available: the endpoint serves one tab per request (the whole-document HTML export is a script shell without the tables, the xlsx export answers 400 — the DGS found the same trying to publish the workbook as one Excel file — and the gviz endpoint is blocked by CORS), and the app must not proxy through any server (FERPA promise; no backend). The sync script was already sequential and keeps its 60 s limit. | 2026-09-05 | Parser accuracy from the DGS's 17 de-identified text transcripts and 3 scans (USC, Buffalo, Northeastern ×3, Duke, IIT, UC San Diego, a Notre Dame official Parchment PDF, a Notre Dame Banner 9 web transcript, Johns Hopkins, a two-column undergraduate record with COPY tiles, UW, SWJTU-Leeds, Georgia Tech, ShanghaiTech, Western Ontario; Ghana, Accra Technical, a US undergraduate scan) | Shared reader (src/transcript/layout.ts, used by BOTH imports): (1) pages are read in their reading orientation — a /Rotate 90 landscape page (Northeastern) and a page whose content is drawn sideways (the official Notre Dame PDF after re-saving) were previously dropped whole as "rotated watermark" (0 courses); (2) a security band drawn as one run repeating a phrase ("DUKE UNIVERSITY ? DUKE UNIVERSITY ? …", UC San Diego's) is dropped, and the phrase it repeats becomes a one-line institution header when it names one; (3) short-word tiles ("COPY" at ≥ 4 x positions, ≥ 8 times) are dropped; (4) two-column detection takes the column's edge as the leftmost x where a substantial share of its runs start (not the leftmost run, not the busiest x) and requires the band before it to be empty. External parser (src/transcript/external.ts): codes spanning two cells with the title in the number's cell (Western "COMPSCI   3331A Title"), dotted numbers with a leading division cell (Johns Hopkins "EN  CSCI  601.226"), two-part subjects ("E E", "A A" at UW), three-letter suffixes (Buffalo "106LEC"), a stray 1–3-letter security mark before the code, credits with three decimals ("3.000"), a bare integer before a decimal credit staying in the title ("Calculus 1   4.000"), a grade before the credits (USC "CSCI-549   B   4.0   Title"), Georgia Tech's one-letter H-column flag dropped, titles after the numbers or on the adjacent plain line (ShanghaiTech; bilingual Chinese records), a numeric token echoing the credits ("3.00   3.00   12.00") never read as a grade, totals lines ("OVERALL 54.47") never read as courses, PeopleSoft's "Term  Course  Transfer Course" table skipped like Banner's transfer block, "Academic Year 2013-2014" as a year header, and A+ mapped to A (the top of Notre Dame's scale — the student can change it). Levels: a cell that is exactly Undergraduate/Graduate, "Student Level : Graduate", a term header ending in the level ("Fall Quarter 2029 Graduate"), "Beginning of UNDERGRADUATE Record", and a closing "GRADUATE SEMESTER TOTALS" line. Degrees: a "Degrees Awarded" block or a "Degree and Date Conferred" table header makes the degree lines under it conferred; the bachelor's date may sit up to four lines below ("Confer Date: …"). University guess: a name spaced across the page ("UNIVERSITY   OF   SOUTHERN   CALIFORNIA"), registrar/provost signature lines and sentences rejected, "Office of the Registrar" / "Course Numbering" / "Grade Scale" tails stripped, "College of …" anywhere treated as a division. Notre Dame parser (src/transcript/parse.ts): Banner 9's Campus column before the Level ("CSE 60641 Main GR Title"), the "Transcript Level" table, the official PDF's "UNIVERSITY OF NOTRE DAME CREDIT:" section and per-record "Course Level:" lines (the record level beats the course number), the transfer block's institution on the term line ("Fall 2020   College Board"), a Roman-numeral title on a credit-only row ("Calculus I   3.000") not read as an Incomplete, A+ → A. Sanitizers: outputs are named sanitized-<seed>.pdf / sanitized-scan-<tag>.* — never after the input, whose name is usually the student's; page rotation is preserved; Banner level/campus codes are kept. Results on the samples: 11/13 transcripts went from 0–20 course rows to complete or near-complete reads; the scans read 11, 28 and 34 rows with OCR-flagged fields. No sanitized file is committed; the layouts are pinned by invented fixtures in tests/layout.test.ts, tests/external-transcript.test.ts and tests/transcript.test.ts. | Claude Code (DGS request 2 of 2026-09-05) | — |

| 2026-09-05 | Deadlines and residence were counted from the wrong term (student bug report: a combined Notre Dame transcript with eight undergraduate semesters showed 13 consecutive semesters, an 8-year limit of 2034-08-15, an 18-month research-qualifier date and a semester-8 candidacy date all off) | Root cause: the app never learned the entry term — "Entered the program" silently defaulted to the fall of the current year, and residence counted every Notre Dame term on the transcript. Fixes: (1) The Notre Dame import READS the entry term. Precedence: a stated admit/matriculation term (the latest, when several curricula state one) → the latest graduate-level term whose Banner block says "Student Type: New…" → the first graduate-level term (rows registered at the graduate level: the transcript's UG/GR column, else the term's level block, else the course number — 6xxxx and up graduate, below 5xxxx undergraduate) → with no graduate-level rows, the first term after the last degree awarded, else the earliest term. The preview offers it as a ticked checkbox ("Set 'Entered the program' to Fall 2024 — the first graduate-level term on your transcript"); the standing card then flags the term as read from the transcript and says that the residency count and every deadline (§4.3 eight-year limit, §4.4.3 eighteen months, §4.4 four semesters, §4.5 eighth semester) are counted from it; touching the dropdown clears the flag. A fresh record's default term is flagged "assumed" the same way. (2) When a Notre Dame degree is awarded with graduate-level terms on both sides of it (a prior Notre Dame M.S. versus an MSCSE earned along the way; a 4+1), the EARLIER reading is kept — earlier deadlines are the safe mistake — and the other reading is spelled out on the preview and the standing card ("if that was a separate degree finished before this program, the entry term is Fall 2026"). (3) Residence (§4.3 / §3.3) counts only terms from the entry term on, in the engine and in the full-time checkboxes. No course counts on that line. While loading, both pages show a progress card (steps per tab, a bar against the 15-second budget, elapsed seconds). Overview cards group courses by core area and by category; retired courses are hidden by default; the rule in effect for the current term is shown when a course has several versions. The footer states the license (© University of Notre Dame du Lac; free non-commercial use; commercial use via the IDEA Center) and links to LICENSE.md and the source. All of it lives in `src/ui/contacts.ts`. | DGS | — |
